### 2.19 `strategy.*`

**Read-surface semantics (assumed).** The
complete named getter surface is pinned by the compiler test catalog;
rows marked `verified` below additionally link value-asserting tests. The
`strategy.*` state/metric getters (`strategy.position_size`,
`strategy.netprofit`, …) are modeled as `series` values reading a per-bar broker
**snapshot** that is frozen at the start of each bar, *after* the prior bar's
order fills but before the script runs. So
within a bar the getter is stable, and history (`strategy.position_size[1]`)
reads the prior bar's snapshot — consistent with every other series read. A
**Note:** under `process_orders_on_close` an order the current bar
places fills at that bar's close (after the snapshot is frozen), so a getter read
later in the *same* bar does not yet reflect that fill; it appears on the next
bar. Getters are compile-gated to `strategy()` scripts (`E0241`) and may be
read inside `if` branches because they are values. Plot-family output calls are
different and remain global-scope-only.
`strategy.avg_trade`/`avg_winning_trade`/`avg_losing_trade`, their percent
forms, `position_avg_price`, `position_entry_name`, and inapplicable margin
values surface `na` when their denominator/position/margin context is empty. The
per-trade `closedtrades.*`/`opentrades.*` accessors read the broker's
retained closed-trade list and open lots by a `trade_num` index (0 = oldest); an
out-of-range index reads `na`, never a panic. `strategy.risk.max_position_size`
caps opened same-direction exposure, `strategy.risk.allow_entry_in` restricts
entry direction, the remaining `strategy.risk.*` halt rules are enforced by the
broker, and `convert_to_account`/`convert_to_symbol` are host-rate currency
interfaces (identity by default). `strategy.default_entry_qty(fill_price)`
exposes the broker's default sizing calculation without placing an order.
Per-trade `profit`, `max_runup`, and `max_drawdown` are marked from per-bar OHLC
excursions; their `_percent` accessors divide by entry notional.

### 2.20 `input.*`

The `input.*` interface follows an **honest host-overridable-defaults** model:
each declaration validates its constant arguments at compile time (`E0230` on
a violation), records an entry in the program's `inputs` metadata table (id,
kind, title, default, constraints, presentation fields, and bounded `active`
metadata), and reads its value at run time from a `RunOptions.inputs` override
map (keyed by input **id**) or the declared default. A host can render controls
and rerun on edits against the same wire contract. `input.source` selects among the
builtin series (default `close`); its result is `series float` (not
`input`-qualified). Every other kind's result carries the `input` qualifier —
the lattice producer this feature introduces. See docs/browser.md
(host-integration) and docs/language-support.md.

| Feature | Status | Tests | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `input()` (generic) | verified | [`input_generic_int_defval_dispatches_like_input_int`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs), [`input_generic_float_defval_dispatches_like_input_float`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs), [`input_generic_bool_string_color_source_dispatch`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs), [`input_generic_named_defval_dispatches`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs) | The overload dispatches on the `defval` literal's type to the matching namespaced input (int→`input.int`, float→`input.float`, bool→`input.bool`, string→`input.string`, color literal / `color.*` constant→`input.color`, a builtin source series ident→`input.source`), then reuses the shared `input.*` machinery (a semantics-level desugar; host-override resolution unchanged). An unclassifiable `defval` is `E0230` (use a namespaced form, e.g. `input.enum`). |
| `input.int()` | verified | [`int_override_changes_run_result`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs), [`ta_input_lengths_compile_once_and_match_changed_defaults`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs), [`ta_length_sweeps_reuse_one_standalone_wasm_instance_and_match_changed_defaults`](../crates/pinewasm_aot/tests/standalone_wasm.rs), [`inputs.pine`](../tests/compile-pass/inputs.pine) | `defval, title, minval, maxval, step, options`; const args, `E0230`. Host overrides of TA integer parameters apply without recompilation; aliases and helper forwarding are covered. Secondary `request.security` input-backed lengths remain unsupported (`E0244`). |
| `input.float()` | verified | [`float_override_changes_run_result`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs), [`float_ta_inputs_and_persistent_aliases_follow_host_overrides`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs), [`runtime_ta_float_inputs_match_recompiled_defaults_and_vm`](../crates/pinewasm_aot/tests/differential.rs), [`runtime_positive_and_percent_ta_float_inputs_match_native_wasm`](../crates/pinewasm_aot/tests/differential.rs) | Full numeric parameter set; an int defval coerces. Direct and unchanged alias inputs in engine-backed TA float parameter positions retain host overrides in the VM and native Wasm. Positive and percentage TA parameter bounds are checked before native execution.  |
| `input.bool()` | verified | [`bool_override_selects_branch`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs) | `defval, title`. |
| `input.source()` | verified | [`source_input_default_and_override_select_series`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs), [`input_source_override_selects_series`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | Selects a builtin or compound source as `series float`. Defaults and overrides preserve the distinct `open`/`high`/`low`/`close`/`hl2`/`hlc3`/`ohlc4`/`hlcc4` identities; the tests assert the exact compound-source formulas. |
| `input.string()` | verified | [`bool_string_color_source_round_trip_metadata`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs), [`input_string_is_not_a_const_plot_title`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs) | `defval, title`; `input string`. Verified: `InputKind::String` with the exact `defval` const-folded into metadata. |

| `input.text_area()` | verified | [`timeframe_session_symbol_text_area_are_string_inputs`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs) | Typed as `input string` (v6). Verified: `InputKind::String` with the exact defval. |
| `input.timeframe()` | partial | [`timeframe_session_symbol_text_area_are_string_inputs`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs) | `input string`. Verified: `InputKind::String` with the exact defval; timeframe-format validation is a documented gap. |
| `input.session()` | partial | [`timeframe_session_symbol_text_area_are_string_inputs`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs) | `input string`. Verified: `InputKind::String` with the exact defval; session-format validation is a documented gap. |
| `input.symbol()` | partial | [`timeframe_session_symbol_text_area_are_string_inputs`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs) | `input string`. Verified: `InputKind::String` with the exact defval; symbol validation is a documented gap. |
| `input.price()` | verified | [`input_price_returns_defval_and_host_override`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | `input.price(defval, ...) → float`. A host-override-or-default interface (`InputKind::Price`), like the other `input.*`: chart click-to-place is a host concern, so it returns the `defval` or a host override. |
| `input.time()` | verified | [`input_time_returns_defval_and_host_override`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | `input.time(defval, ...) → int` (epoch ms). Host-override-or-default (`InputKind::Time`); the `defval` or a host override. |
| `input.enum()` | verified | [`input_enum_defaults_to_member_and_host_override_selects_another`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs), [`input_enum_non_member_defval_is_rejected`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/inputs.rs) | `input.enum(defval, ...)` binds an enum-typed default to the declaring `enum`. The `defval` is an enum member `Name.member`, resolved against the collected enums; the new `InputKind::Enum` + `InputValue::Enum { name, index }` carry it, the read expression types as `Type::Enum(name)` (`input`-qualified), and its runtime value is the member's variant index (the same `int` any enum member lowers to). A host override supplies another member (name + index). A non-member `defval` is `E0230`. Parser: `enum` doubles as an `input.*` namespace segment (like the type keywords). |

### 2.22 `ticker.*`

PineWASM provides a `simple` and a `series` overload of every `ticker.*` function and of the `syminfo.prefix()` / `syminfo.ticker()` function forms. PineWASM accepts `series` arguments for every symbol, ticker id, session, adjustment, style, numeric and `request_wicks` parameter; the result is then a per-bar `series string` ([`ticker_functions_accept_series_arguments_and_evaluate_per_bar`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/v6_series_accessors.rs), [`ticker_results_keep_the_qualifier_of_their_arguments`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/v6_series_accessors.rs)). The `backadjustment` and `settlement_as_close` selector tokens are `simple` in both overloads, so a `series` token stays `E0206` ([`ticker_selector_tokens_stay_simple`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/v6_series_accessors.rs)).

| Feature | Status | Tests | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ticker.new()` | verified | [`ticker_heikinashi_returns_deterministic_string`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`ticker_optional_arguments_fill_gaps_without_changing_trailing_ids`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`backadjustment_and_settlement_consts_are_usable_in_ticker_new`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`v6_parameter_names_are_accepted_as_named_arguments`](../crates/pinewasm_semantics/src/tests.rs) | `ticker.new(prefix, ticker, session?, adjustment?, backadjustment?, settlement_as_close?) → string`. Folds supplied values into `new:<prefix>:<ticker>[:…]`; named later arguments materialize empty interior gaps while trailing omissions preserve existing ids. `backadjustment.*` and `settlement_as_close.*` are opaque selector tokens. `na` propagates. **Note:** real symbology resolution is a host concern — the canonical id is opaque, not a symbol. |

Optional-gap and trailing-omission behavior matches `ticker.new`. Host-symbology known limit. |

### 2.25 `timeframe.*`

| Feature | Status | Tests | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `timeframe.change()` | partial | [`timeframe_change_opens_calendar_months_quarters_and_years`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`timeframe_change_opens_monday_weeks`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`timeframe_change_walks_fixed_widths_on_the_exchange_clock`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`timeframe_change_follows_the_trading_day_lane`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`timeframe_change_is_time_opening_a_new_bucket`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`timeframe_change_reads_a_const_or_input_timeframe_as_the_literal`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`timeframe_change_nested_in_its_own_timeframe_compiles`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`timeframe_change_accepts_an_input_timeframe`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/v6_invalid_parameter_diagnostics.rs), [`timeframe_change_accepts_a_computed_timeframe_in_a_lazy_branch`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/v6_invalid_parameter_diagnostics.rs), [`timeframe_change_with_a_switching_timeframe_compares_bucket_opening_times`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/v6_invalid_parameter_diagnostics.rs), [`timeframe_change_with_a_runtime_timeframe_keeps_per_call_state_like_the_literal`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/v6_invalid_parameter_diagnostics.rs), [`timeframe_change_with_an_input_timeframe_matches_the_literal_on_realtime_ticks`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/v6_invalid_parameter_diagnostics.rs), [`timeframe_change_with_a_runtime_timeframe_matches_the_literal_in_vm_and_wasm`](../crates/pinewasm_aot/tests/differential.rs), [`timeframe_change_with_a_const_timeframe_compiles_as_the_literal`](../crates/pinewasm_aot/tests/differential.rs), [`a_fixed_timeframe_change_matches_the_interpreter`](../crates/pinewasm_aot/tests/standalone_wasm_chart_time.rs), [`a_calendar_timeframe_change_still_needs_a_host`](../crates/pinewasm_aot/tests/standalone_wasm_chart_time.rs), [`a_blank_timeframe_change_is_the_chart_period`](../crates/pinewasm_aot/tests/standalone_wasm_chart_time.rs) | `true` on the first chart bar of each `timeframe` bucket: exactly when `time(timeframe)` opens a new one. Calendar months, quarters and years start on the first trading day, weeks on Monday (multi-week blocks restart at the year's first Monday), and fixed widths follow the exchange clock, the buckets `request.security` resamples into. Limitation: a day (and a fixed width of a day or more) opens at the exchange's midnight; futures sessions that open the evening before are not modelled. A timeframe no longer than the chart's makes every bar a change. `timeframe` is typed as `series string`; a literal, `const`, input or computed one all lower onto `time(timeframe)` and a hidden `var int` holding the opening time the call last saw, so a timeframe that changes between bars reports a change when the new bucket opens at a different time. A non-string is `E0205`. A standalone WASM module serves a fixed-width literal itself (on the UTC clock, which the module assumes); a calendar week or month keeps `host.vm`. |
| `timeframe.from_seconds()` | verified | [`timeframe_in_seconds_and_from_seconds_round_trip`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`context_accessors.pine`](../tests/compile-pass/context_accessors.pine) | `int -> string`. Canonical form: whole days `"<n>D"`, whole minutes `"<n>"`, else `"<n>S"`. **assumed** canonical choice. |
| `timeframe.in_seconds()` | partial | [`timeframe_in_seconds_and_from_seconds_round_trip`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`context_accessors.pine`](../tests/compile-pass/context_accessors.pine) | Reuses `timeframe_in_seconds`; `"1W"` == 604800. An omitted timeframe uses the current chart timeframe; explicit and named arguments retain their behavior. Default-context regression: [`timeframe_seconds_default_tracks_market_timeframe`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/named_args.rs). |
| `timeframe.isdaily` | verified | [`timeframe_period_and_isdaily_default_to_one_day`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`context_accessors.pine`](../tests/compile-pass/context_accessors.pine) | `bool` derived from the chart timeframe (default `"1D"` → true). |
| `timeframe.isdwm` | verified | [`timeframe_period_and_isdaily_default_to_one_day`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | `bool`, true for days/weeks/months. |
| `timeframe.isintraday` | verified | [`timeframe_predicates_track_the_chart_timeframe`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`context_accessors.pine`](../tests/compile-pass/context_accessors.pine) | `bool`, true for seconds/minutes. |
| `timeframe.isminutes` | verified | [`timeframe_predicates_track_the_chart_timeframe`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | `bool`; a bare-number timeframe (`"5"`) is minutes. |
| `timeframe.ismonthly` | verified | [`timeframe_predicates_track_the_chart_timeframe`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | `bool`, true for a `"<n>M"` timeframe. |
| `timeframe.isseconds` | verified | [`timeframe_predicates_track_the_chart_timeframe`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | `bool`, true for a `"<n>S"` timeframe. |
| `timeframe.isticks` | partial | [`timeframe_predicates_track_the_chart_timeframe`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | `bool`, always false — this VM has no tick timeframes. **assumed**. |
| `timeframe.isweekly` | verified | [`timeframe_predicates_track_the_chart_timeframe`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | `bool`, true for a `"<n>W"` timeframe. |
| `timeframe.main_period` | partial | [`timeframe_period_and_isdaily_default_to_one_day`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | `string`; equals `timeframe.period` here (no secondary contexts). |
| `timeframe.multiplier` | verified | [`timeframe_period_and_isdaily_default_to_one_day`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`context_accessors.pine`](../tests/compile-pass/context_accessors.pine) | `int` numeric part of the timeframe (1 for `"1D"`, 30 for `"30S"`). |
| `timeframe.period` | verified | [`timeframe_period_and_isdaily_default_to_one_day`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`context_accessors.pine`](../tests/compile-pass/context_accessors.pine) | `string`, the chart timeframe (default `"1D"`). |

The chart-timeframe values and predicates above carry Pine's `simple` qualifier. Reading their history promotes the expression to `series`; [`timeframe_values_are_simple_until_history_is_read`](../crates/pinewasm_semantics/src/tests.rs) pins that distinction. This lets chart-timeframe choices supply a legal `simple int` length to `ta.ema`.

If an older program gives one of these four a set that the engine resolves after the run, and the set holds the screener destination but is not `display.all`, the VM hides the output and adds an entry for `display` to `DisplayStyle.unsupported` ([`runtime_resolved_screener_only_level_is_hidden_with_a_diagnostic`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/v6_display_arithmetic_variables.rs)). |

### 2.30 Newer v6 namespaces

Additional v6 namespaces. The
`footprint.*()` and `volume_row.*()` functions read the `footprint` and
`volume_row` **object types** produced by `request.footprint()` (§2.10), now
implemented as **host-or-default interfaces**: `footprint` and `volume_row` are
registered object types (like the drawing object types), `request.footprint()`
materializes a `footprint` from the optional `MarketData::footprint_data` host
table, and every accessor is a field read that returns the host value or
`na`/empty/`false` by default (no real order-flow feed — data sourcing is a host
concern, in the same style as the other `request.*` interfaces). The names and signatures
are implemented as listed; behavior that the entry
does not fully specify remains explicitly marked as assumed. The
`backadjustment.*` and `settlement_as_close.*` selector
namespaces are implemented as const-string tokens accepted by
`ticker.new()`/`ticker.modify()`; the functions preserve them in their opaque
canonical ids, while applying the selected market adjustment remains a host
concern. The three constant namespaces are grouped one row per member set,
matching the `currency.*` convention in §2.29.

## 3. Runtime idiosyncrasies

The behaviors below are where PineWASM's specific choices affect results.
Each row states what PineWASM does today and whether that behavior is *assumed*
(the test pins PineWASM's own chosen behavior).

| Behavior | Status | Tests | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| NA arithmetic (na operand → na result) | verified | [`na_propagates_through_arithmetic_and_comparison`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | na propagates through arithmetic and comparison. |
| NA comparison (na operand → false) | verified | [`slots_history_mixed_pairs_missing_and_exact_large_ints_keep_their_boundaries`](../crates/pinewasm_aot/tests/float_comparison_tolerance.rs) | Missing operands produce ordinary false, including `!=`; covered in both orientations on VM and standalone native routes. |
| `and`/`or`: Kleene logic with short-circuit evaluation | verified | [`logic_follows_kleene_semantics`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`or_short_circuits_a_true_lhs_before_a_faulting_rhs`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`and_short_circuits_a_false_lhs_before_a_faulting_rhs`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`lazy_or_skips_a_stateful_ta_call_in_the_rhs`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | **Resolved**: evaluation is lazy — a decisive lhs skips the rhs, so guarded faults and stateful rhs effects are not evaluated. Truth tables are unchanged (strong Kleene); an `na` lhs still evaluates the rhs. |
| na `if`-condition handling (treated as false) | verified | [`na_propagates_through_arithmetic_and_comparison`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | **Assumed.** |
| int/float promotion | verified | [`mixed_division_is_float`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`int_comparisons_and_mixed_comparisons`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`mixed_arithmetic_types_as_float_with_visible_coercion`](../crates/pinewasm_semantics/src/tests.rs) | Mixed int/float operands promote the int side to float via an explicit compile-time coercion; int-int stays statically int for `+ - * / %`, while `/` can retain a fractional runtime value. `bar_index` is series int. |
| Division semantics (÷0, overflow, non-finite results → na) | verified | [`division_by_zero_is_na`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`division_and_modulo_by_zero_are_na`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`int_overflow_is_na`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`non_finite_market_data_reads_as_na`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | `int / int` keeps its fraction; int rules (checked overflow → na, `% 0` → na) are **assumed** and pinned by tests; float edge cases (0/0) beyond the tested ones remain assumed. |
| `var` persistence across bars | verified | [`var_counter_increments_across_bars`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`var_persists_without_reassignment`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`conditional_var_initializes_once_on_first_execution`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | Carry-forward + per-slot written flag (initialize-once). Conditional first-execution timing is **assumed** and pinned by test. On realtime reruns, ordinary `var` state rolls back to the bar-start snapshot while `varip` remains intrabar-persistent. |
| `varip` intrabar persistence | verified | [`varip_counts_ticks_var_counts_bars`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | `varip` state is excluded from the realtime snapshot/restore, so it persists across intrabar ticks. The realtime test drives a 3-tick forming bar: the `varip` counter advances once per tick (`1, 2, 5`) while the `var` counter rolls back and commits once per bar (`1, 2, 3`). |
| Realtime bar rollback and closing-update commit | verified | [`varip_counts_ticks_var_counts_bars`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`ta_sma_state_rolls_back_across_ticks`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`incremental_ticks_retain_exact_log_events_while_state_rolls_back`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/realtime_log_retention_tests.rs) | `RunOptions.realtime` reruns a forming bar from the prior committed state. Ordinary `var`/TA state rolls back; `varip` and emitted logs survive. The closing update commits ordinary state for the next bar. These tests cover their declared finite tick sequences. |
| `max_bars_back` sizing / "too far back" runtime errors | verified | [`offset_beyond_hard_limit_is_e0233`](../crates/pinewasm_semantics/src/tests.rs), [`offset_beyond_hard_limit_is_e0233_even_when_declared`](../crates/pinewasm_semantics/src/tests.rs), [`dynamic_history_offset_beyond_history_is_na`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`dynamic_builtin_history_offset_beyond_max_bars_back_errors`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`dynamic_user_slot_history_offset_beyond_max_bars_back_errors`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | A literal offset past the 5000-bar buffer limit is reported as `E0233`, and a dynamic offset whose non-negative value exceeds the effective ceiling is a structured `HistoryBufferLimitExceeded` runtime error. Dynamic offsets within the ceiling but before bar 0 yield `false` for Boolean history and `na` otherwise. |
| Global-scope-only effect calls (`plot` etc.) | verified | [`plot_family_calls_in_local_scope_are_rejected_with_e0254_and_full_call_spans`](../crates/pinewasm_semantics/src/tests.rs), [`plot_family_calls_at_global_scope_remain_valid`](../crates/pinewasm_semantics/src/tests.rs), [`plot_in_local_scope_is_still_rejected_when_captured`](../crates/pinewasm_compiler/tests/fills.rs) | Plot-family calls inside an `if` branch, loop, or user function are rejected with `E0254`; the diagnostic spans the complete call and rejected calls allocate no output metadata. The same calls remain valid at global scope. A script that needs conditional output computes the series in local control flow and passes it to one top-level output call. |
| `calc_on_every_tick` / bar-close vs. tick execution | partial | [`indicator_rejects_calc_on_every_tick`](../crates/pinewasm_semantics/src/tests.rs), [`strategy_full_param_surface_const_folds`](../crates/pinewasm_semantics/src/tests.rs), [`varip_counts_ticks_var_counts_bars`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | Accepted on strategies; rejected on indicators. Indicators execute on realtime updates supplied by the host. Tick and rollback behaviour is covered by the linked tests only. |
| Repainting behaviors (lookahead, security on higher TF) | verified | [`security_close_weekly_lookahead_on_repaints_forming_bucket`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs) | `request.security` on a higher timeframe with `barmerge.lookahead_on` repaints: mid-bucket bars see the *forming* higher-TF value (the not-yet-closed aggregate), so historical results differ from what would be known in realtime. See runtime.md. |
| Resource limits (instructions/bar, stack depth, max bars) | verified | [`instruction_limit_is_enforced`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`max_bars_limits_execution`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`run_options_default_uses_the_public_per_bar_budget`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs), [`emitted_prepare_initializes_the_reference_per_bar_budget`](../crates/pinewasm_aot/tests/differential.rs) | PineWASM-specific, not part of v6: defaults 64,000,000 instructions/bar, 4096 stack depth; structured `RuntimeError` with bar index and span ([`runtime_errors_carry_spans`](../crates/pinewasm_vm/src/tests.rs)). |

## 4. Update policy

- **Status rule:** status changes ship with the code and tests that justify them. A change
  that implements a feature but does not update this file (or vice versa) is incomplete.
- **Test-link rule:** promoting a row to `supported`/`verified` requires adding the
  covering test link in the same edit. Demotions (regressions, removed features) must
  also land here immediately.
- **Assumed rows:** when a test covers an assumed behavior,
  drop the "assumed" note and, if our behavior differs, either fix the code or promote
  the note to an explicit **Limitation**.
- **Unverified names:** rows flagged *"unverified name — confirm against the linked tests"* should be checked against the linked tests and either confirmed
  (drop the flag) or corrected/removed.
- **CI enforcement:** `cargo xtask coverage-check`, backed by the Rust-native
  `pinewasm_xtask`, parses this file in
  `cargo xtask check`, recomputes the summary table, verifies linked evidence and test
  symbols, synchronizes the README counts, and checks the diagnostic registry.
